==> docs/CASE_WORKSHOP.md <==
# Case Workshop proof of concept

Open **More > Case Workshop** in the game for the **live case editor**. It loads the
current board's original evidence, retains a draft for the current case, and files cases
that can be presented privately, on the meeting floor, or during nomination speech.
Read the [live workflow and fallback limits](CASE_WORKSHOP_INTEGRATION.md#live-canvas).
The experimental reading evaluator remains opt-in; the screen labels unsupported cards,
whole-case fallback and actual listener outcomes. Opening it does not enable Debug.

This document's remaining sections describe the **separate frozen experiment**. Open
`ui/case_workshop.tscn` in Godot and run that scene with F6 to use it.

The Workshop freezes a scripted ten-person town at dawn on day 3. It tests expressing,
interpreting and revising a case. Its new interpreter does not change the main game's
argument, persuasion, voting or dialogue systems. The separate
[live integration experiment](CASE_WORKSHOP_INTEGRATION.md) has an opt-in restricted
shared ledger; the F6 fixture canvas still runs independently of the main case.

**Preview effectiveness** calculates an on-demand response from a neutral ghost juror.
It shows approximate effects for everyone and intermediate claim acceptance without
changing the case or remembered mock jury. The ghost has no private knowledge or known
role; its declared neutral inputs are shown with the result. This is a reference for case
strength, not a prediction of a particular NPC or a probability of guilt. The live comparison
panel also has a player-safe version that does not require Debug.

Slices 1–5 of the [implementation plan](CASE_WORKSHOP_PLAN.md) are implemented within
their bounded fixture scope. The first three provide:
explicit claims/citations, report conflicts, multiple reasoning lines, and shared
support, followed by bounded Insight reasoning and experimental confidence persistence.
The controlled soft-factor experiment tests convergence; actual movement and trace
likelihoods are available in the separate Slice-4 experiment. A pre-Slice-4 step adds scoped intermediate-claim
assessments and one replaceable set of effects on everyone implicated or exonerated.

The default screen is a freeform card canvas with four selectable experiments based
on fixture 692. The previous
list-based experiment remains available by running `ui/case_workshop_lab.tscn` with F6.
It retains the seed generator and supplied-factor controls. Earlier experiment formats
are refused because their belief-update semantics differ.

## Canvas workflow

Use the selector beside **Make a Case** for **692 · Reading chains**, **Movement & soot**,
**Poison & recovery**, or **Revenant & restoration**. See the
[experiment guide](CASE_WORKSHOP_EXPERIMENTS.md) for the new controls, models and limits.
Changing experiments starts a fresh workspace; save arrangements you want to retain.

1. The workspace starts with all sixteen evidence cards and a Constance accusation.
   **Load mockup** assembles the user's proposed reading chain and challenged alibi.
   The reading answers stay unknown so the alternative Constance/Nisha explanations
   remain available to explore. **All evidence** fits the scratch cards; **Fit case**
   fits the selected card and its incoming argument.
2. Drag a card with the left button. Drag empty space or use the middle button to pan;
   use the wheel to zoom. **Answer** opens a separate editor. Answers remain on the
   card and are shared wherever that evidence is used. Closing the editor keeps edits.
3. **Add Case Premise** creates a claimed conclusion or a generic **Supporting evidence**
   group. A group's claim is optional. Its incoming evidence supplies the argument;
   its intermediate conclusion never becomes an additional accepted fact. **Answer**
   on a premise edits its assumed alignments/Marked identities. Root assumptions apply
   to the whole selected case; supporting premises contribute their local assumptions.
   Conflicting assumptions, including inside a nested branch, are reported.
4. Click **Link**, then a destination card. Arrows point from support to the thing it
   supports. Evidence-to-evidence links normally mean **read together**. An applicable
   walking-role claim linked to a sighting defaults to **could explain outing**, using
   the same citation checks as the list editor. Right-click switches applicable role
   links between context and an outing explanation; an explicit outing answer takes
   precedence over the default. A home alibi and same-night observation
   that its subject was out automatically form a merged red contradiction card.
5. **Inspect** a contradiction to see both originals and its interpretation. It means
   that the observation challenges the home alibi. If the observation is accurate,
   the alibi is false; deliberate lying and evil are further inferences. Right-click
   **Unlink merged contradiction** restores the originals, retaining their answers and
   references. Ordinary connections can also be removed through the right-click menu.
   Premises can be removed while retaining their evidence. Circular links are refused.
6. Select a premise with a claim and **Present selected premise**. Only its incoming
   argument is presented. Disconnected cards remain scratch work. A conflict premise
   can ask whether the original accounts conflict, without first resolving which is
   wrong or assigning alignment guilt. The mock jury opens in its own window, with
   the existing trust, Insight, confidence and comparison controls. Close/reopen it
   using **Mock jury**. Remembered discussion still replaces this one case's prior
   effects on every character; it does not maintain several independent case ledgers.
   **Effects on everyone** shows collateral changes and retractions. **Claims and their
   connections** shows each scoped inference, its doubts, unattended evidence, and
   whether its evidence strengthens the parent or leaves an alternative available.
7. **Save/Open** stores the graph, card positions, answers, active premise, view, jury
   settings and replayable discussion. Canvas files use `demonologist-workshop-canvas-1`,
   containing the version-5 experiment. Imports validate provenance and graph structure
   before replacing the current workspace. Older histories require their original code
   version; they are not silently replayed under the new model.

Merrin linked through Bart/Fenwick and all three linked directly to the Hollis premise
have the same evidence roots and assessment. Shared paths do not duplicate a witness.
Rearranging cards has no effect. Explicit grouping can still affect which connections a
bounded-Insight juror attends to; named source trust and gift explanations retain their
distinct roles. Links support editing and attention, not an ordering-dependent evidence bonus.

The original reading experiment's added evidence-weighting rule is the challenged home alibi:
one fixed, uncalibrated **2× evil/good comparison factor**, gated by the contradicting
observation's credibility. The quoted alibi records known testimony without assuming
its contents true; distrusting Constance does not erase the fact that she made the claim.
Repeated references to the same outing share this factor. It can favor Constance while
both logical alignment alternatives remain possible. Its value is exposed in Inspect
and in the jury calculation; it is not a general likelihood model for deception.

## Intermediate claims and collateral consequences (pre-Slice-4 step)

A Constance headline no longer limits the outcome to Constance. With the three core
readings, Hollis and Lian can gain suspicion while Constance versus Nisha remains
unresolved. Reliable reports can also exonerate people. A juror can acknowledge that
Hollis follows **if the readings are sound**, while doubting the witnesses enough to
remain only partly persuaded. Logical implication and social acceptance stay distinct.

Each named premise is assessed using its own incoming evidence and assumptions, within
the presentation's shared attention window. Claim-less groups preserve nested named
premises. The NPC can dispute an incorrect intermediate claim and explain why its
connection does not settle the parent. The label itself never becomes an extra report
or a sequential suspicion bonus. Original observations can remain relevant even when
the player's interpretation of them is mistaken; there is no hard all-or-nothing gate
that discards them merely because an intermediate claim is rejected.

For every person, the experiment compares support for evil and good using the same
starting attitudes, source credibility, reading accuracy and attended evidence. It
subtracts the comparison already supported by private reports and prior dispositions.
The provisional alignment signal is `0.45 * (evil evidence change - good evidence change)`.
This prevents a merely possible accusation from automatically increasing everyone's
suspicion and prevents repackaging the juror's own known reading as fresh evidence.
The reference subtraction is an experimental disposition comparison, not a posterior
probability or a calibrated measure of information.

The earlier outing-pattern heuristic remains available. Because it can describe the
same incident as the challenged-alibi factor, the experiment takes the stronger absolute
alignment/pattern route for that person, **never their sum**, then applies player trust
and confidence once. This conservative envelope can hide a weaker independent route;
it is deliberately temporary pending Slice 4's shared causal movement/trace model.
The inspector distinguishes headline conviction from this actual signed effect.
An explicit conflict question still assigns no alignment effects.

Claim-to-parent diagnostics compare the parent with and without the branch's exclusive
original evidence. Sources that also reach the parent through another path remain
included. These differences are not additive contributions, and no extra persuasion is
awarded for an accepted intermediate claim or a positive connection diagnostic. Grouping
can affect bounded attention, but projections never receive extra unattended public
cards. Each conclusion has the existing bounded search budget; this is not a single
fixed total computation budget for an entire multi-conclusion presentation.

The ledger records the actual clamped effect on each character. Before any revision,
the whole old vector is retracted; all new consequences are computed from that frozen
baseline and applied together. Hollis cannot become a less credible source halfway
through the same presentation. Repetition, reused roots, duplicate derivations and
switching the headline cannot stack effects. Independent simultaneous case ledgers and
cross-case provenance remain future work.

Board-to-workshop transfer, independent duplicate interpretations, collapsing whole
cases, relationship-choice prompts for ambiguous future evidence, and automatic
movement/soot inference from the running game remain future work. The separate movement
experiment is implemented; this canvas preloads controlled fixture evidence,
it does not read or alter the running main-game case. A linked role claim does not
silently accept its associated gift result.

## List lab experiments

1. **Load example > Surveyor only.** Leave slots unknown and present the case. A partial
   explanation is allowed. Accepting the Surveyor claim assumes the source was good,
   held that gift, and was healthy at the reading's time; good does not exclude Marked.
2. **Add Rumormonger**, then **Three core readings**. In seed 692 there are two worlds:
   Constance/Hollis/Lian evil with Nisha Marked, or Nisha/Hollis/Lian evil with Constance
   Marked. These are compatible assignments, not equally likely outcomes or probabilities.
   Argue that Hollis is evil to compare a forced conclusion with the unresolved Constance
   accusation.
3. Answer the Rumormonger card with Nisha, or choose **Good · Marked** for Nisha under
   **Assumed alignments & Marked**. That narrows your proposed explanation. The jury can
   acknowledge the conditional inference without treating your selection as testimony.
4. Leave **Continue remembered discussion** off to compare from identical starting
   attitudes. Turn it on to present, revise, and present again. Remove a core report;
   the earlier effect is replaced, including retraction when its support disappears.
5. Load **Repeated night outings**. Add your vigil: the same night remains one incident.
   Answer **Night gift** on a Watch card, then choose a specific supporting claim in
   the picker below it. Agnes's Lamplighter claim is separately available: citing it
   does not accept her count. Wrong-person, non-walking and wrong-period claims grant
   no discount. Inspect any report's associated role claim from the evidence column.
6. Load **Conflicting reports**. The home alibi and sighting cannot both be accurate.
   Select the specific reports in the conflict scope. Minimal conflicting sets appear
   in the case check; keep one as a derived question or optionally name the report
   you question. The jury stores accuracy weights for the reports, without assigning
   alignment guilt. Naming a report as wrong supplies no new evidence. Derived
   questions refer to the originals and cannot multiply their effect.
7. Under **Adjust this juror**, vary confidence, trust in you, or trust/suspicion toward
   a report's source. **Show calculation & accepted path** exposes agreement, premise
   quality, confidence mode and the actual old/new ledger effects. The original mode
   scales attitude change, not the logical result. Restore the generated jury or reset
   discussion as needed.
8. Set the conclusion to **Hollis is evil**, compare Surveyor + Rumormonger with all
   three readings, and inspect the calculation. Both force Hollis conditionally, but
   the second sufficient route now adds support. Merrin is a shared dependency, not
   two independent witnesses. The inspector shows the old strongest-path score too.
9. Load **Converging lines (test factor)**. Select a line to edit its title, evidence,
   assumptions, answers and optional intermediate claim. Supporting lines combine;
   alternative explanations are checked separately. Unassigned selected evidence
   remains part of the whole case. Intermediate claims are diagnostics, not new facts.
   The displayed test ratio is a supplied input: 1× is neutral, above 1 favors evil
   for its selected person, below 1 favors good. Change the target to Nisha and favor
   good to see support move indirectly toward Constance through the reading chain.
   Both logical alternatives can remain possible while conviction changes.
10. Under **Adjust this juror**, change one reading's accuracy separately from trust
    in its source. Doubting Bart's reading preserves the Dowser/Rumormonger route;
    doubting Merrin's reading weakens both. Even a juror's own reading can be questioned
    without denying that they received that result.
11. Load **Insight comparison**, or choose **Compare Insight levels** under any juror.
    This compares full reasoning with Low, Medium and High Insight using the same juror,
    private knowledge and starting attitudes. It does not change the discussion history.
    Under **Adjust this juror**, choose **Bounded reasoning (Insight)** to use that model
    for presentations. Inspect the explanation checks, rival checks, ignored evidence
    and ignored lines. A coherent explanation without a rival search is provisional.
12. Choose **Confidence sustains a theory (experiment)** and enable **Continue remembered
    discussion**. Present, then revise the evidence or supplied factor and present again.
    The inspector shows the remembered partial position and whether it was retained or
    revised. Confidence no longer scales the incoming effect in this mode. **Reset
    discussion** starts a fresh theory. Use the original confidence mode for comparison.

There are up to six reasoning lines and eight derived questions per case. Duplicate
or out-of-domain slots are blocked; unknown slots remain valid. Conflicting hypotheses
can still be presented. Supporting lines cannot quietly use incompatible identities.
Repeated references share their underlying fact/incident identities. Two witnesses to
one outing can support its occurrence without making it two outings. Overlapping or
contradictory supplied test factors receive explicit feedback instead of multiplying.

**Save experiment** exports the seed, public evidence, current case/version, jury settings,
each presentation, objections and actual changes. **Open experiment** validates the file
and replays its history rather than trusting the exported outcomes. Imports support up to
200 presentations. Developer truth is omitted unless its checkbox is enabled; that data
never reaches the interpreter. Version 5 includes intermediate-claim scopes, dependency
links, multi-character effects, movement models, poison hypotheses and chronology in
the earlier provenance and reasoning
settings. Personal knowledge belongs to the fixture; theories are reconstructed
from presentations, not accepted as injected baseline memory. Version-1 through version-4
exports are explicitly refused because the assessment model changed; they are not
silently replayed with different semantics.

## Scenario and evidence boundaries

- Seed 692 is a teaching fixture inspired by case 692, **not a replay of that trace**.
  Agnes has the Lamplighter gift and Greta the Veteran gift so the five chosen evidence
  types coexist in one legal lineup. The five types are Surveyor, Rumormonger, Dowser,
  Night Watch and Lamplighter. Sixteen cards include associated role claims, a separate
  Warden claim, an alibi and player sighting. The quiet night in this fixture is a
  Veteran survival, not confirmed corroboration of the Warden claim.
- Other seeds permute cast assignments within this same scenario structure. They are
  reproducible variations, not an unrestricted three-day campaign generator. The fixtures
  deliberately retain the four-block Surveyor result and the core Marked ambiguity.
- `workshop/scenario.gd` constructs real World/NPC objects. It uses the existing
  `_receive_info` and `_day_gifts` routines, rejection-sampling the teaching reports.
  Night routes and the two legal Veteran attacks are scripted; full night AI is not run.
- Role, alignment and registration are separate. There are three original evil people
  and exactly one good Marked person. Marked registration can differ between readings.
  Reading/event time, reporting time and learned time are separate fields. In this
  fixture testimony is reported when the player acquires it. Role assertions retain
  whether they were implicit in a report, their origin and their asserted interval.
- The original reading fixture has no alignment transitions, poisoner, or role changes. The
  exported alignment epoch explicitly covers opening through day 3. Revenant/Scion
  transitions now have separate bounded experiments. Arbitrary histories, Scion
  succession and the remaining roles require further work.
- The movement solver checks possible sets of walkers and the Lamplighter's count,
  excluding the Lamplighter. Watch sightings require those people to be out. It does not
  solve alternative routes or infer that an unseen person stayed home. Full route/alibi
  reasoning and physical trace patterns remain outside this slice.

## Interpreter shape and provisional tuning

The tuning discussion below describes the original reading fixture. The new joint
movement and temporal experiments extend it as documented in the
[experiment guide](CASE_WORKSHOP_EXPERIMENTS.md).

`case + listener + context -> assessment + ledger update + response`

`workshop/solver.gd` enumerates compatible alignment/Marked assignments and separate
night-outing assignments. Selected reliable reports constrain their sources to good at
the report time. Player answers constrain the proposed explanation; implication before
those answers is reported separately. Duplicates and impossible selections get feedback;
the Workshop lets the player present a mistaken case.

`workshop/case_graph.gd` compiles supporting lines to one case, expands derived questions
to their evidence roots and checks incompatible assumptions. Alternative explanations
are isolated. Its output never treats an intermediate conclusion as an independent premise.

`workshop/support_model.gd` evaluates a mixture of credibility scenarios. A shared source
variable gates that source's reports; a separate accuracy variable can disable a particular
reading. Disabling a report removes its constraint, rather than asserting its opposite.
Each consistent scenario contributes once, so overlapping proof routes do not duplicate
Merrin. Inconsistent scenarios are excluded and remaining weights normalized, exposing
localized report-accuracy doubt. These are experimental tuning weights, not calibrated
probabilities or a full generative Bayesian model. A source variable is a coarse shared
dependency; bounded poison periods and conversion histories are now tested in the
separate experiments. General causal histories remain future work.

The jury retains player trust, confidence, stance, suspicion and a replaceable evidence
ledger. Private knowledge of being good is fixed; own readings participate through their
accuracy settings. For unresolved cases, strongest supporting/rival worlds are ranked
using suspicion/trust terms shaped like `Argue._expl_weight`. Supplied soft factors modify
those rankings and can favor either alternative. Counts of worlds never become probabilities.
The inspector retains the previous strongest-path calculation for comparison.

### Bounded Insight reasoning

Full reasoning remains the default comparison mode. Bounded reasoning constructs and
checks candidate explanations independently; it does not take the referee's surviving
worlds and pretend to have searched them. The deterministic profiles are experimental:

| Insight | Presented cards | Connected lines | Explanation checks | Rival checks |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Low, below 0.34 | 3 | 1 | 8 | 0 |
| Medium, below 0.67 | 5 | 2 | 48 | 16 nearby swaps |
| High | 10 | 6 | 840 | 840, exhaustive when necessary |

These are upper limits, not work the juror must always perform. Source trust, count
readings and references to the target affect attention priority. Duplicate line wrappers
share one attention group. Own reports remain available outside the card limit. A juror
can remember sightings while missing their connection to another line; the inspector
shows ignored lines as well as ignored cards. Ignored evidence cannot silently add
outing suspicion or cancel conviction through the referee's more complete assessment.

The search can find a coherent proposal, a rival, a conditional implication, or remain
unresolved. Medium Insight tests swaps from a found proposal; exhausting those swaps
does not prove uniqueness. Only exhaustive rival absence can certify an implication,
and only under the attended premises. A failed limited search is not disproof. The
referee's logical answer is independent of Insight and stays visible as a diagnostic.
Spoken alternatives come only from the juror's own search.

Direct personal conflicts are checked even outside attention: being good at this epoch,
what result the juror actually received, and their known whereabouts. Receipt does not
guarantee a healthy gift result. A rejected report is disabled individually, without
automatically discrediting its source's other reports. A direct home/sighting conflict
within attended evidence is also recognized. This fixture has no Sleepwalker or personal
conversion history; those exceptions still require slice 5.

The displayed search grants reports with source weight at least 0.4 and reading accuracy
at least 0.5. Numeric conviction separately averages the credibility states, running a
bounded search in each. A checked but unchallenged proposal starts at 0.3 with one active
core reading or 0.76 with two or more, before credibility and supplied-factor adjustments.
This deliberately exposes a tunable limited-search acceptance rule; it is not a probability
or a general measure of argument strength. Distrusted sources still weaken agreement.

### Confidence as a provisional commitment

The original mode retains the incoming-effect multiplier below. The alternative mode
uses a multiplier of 1 and remembers a partial commitment about the target at the
alignment epoch, with a separate Marked question. It compares at most the supporting
and rival candidates found in the displayed search, rather than deriving everyone's
suspicion from an arbitrary completed world. Initial adoption has no persistence bonus.

On changed evidence, a retained commitment biases hypothesis ranking by `3^confidence`.
A contrary candidate can dislodge it at comparison odds of `1 + 4 * confidence`; direct
personal facts and an exhaustive opposite implication without extra player choices can
override it. Low confidence can also rebuild provisionally when the prior alternative
is unavailable. Source trust and reading accuracy do not change, and confidence cannot
invent a poisoning excuse. Repeating identical inputs reuses the decision; it cannot
incrementally reinforce the theory. Removing the evidence retracts the case's numerical
effect even if a remembered theory label remains.

This is an experiment in per-target commitments during revision of one editable case,
not an autonomous, coherent theory of the whole town or production NPC integration.

The initial tuning is deliberately visible and experimental:

| Component | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| Forced implication in a credibility scenario | agreement 1.0, weighted once by that scenario |
| Conditional on player choices | agreement 0.4 inside that scenario; does not promote a chosen conclusion into its own premise |
| Merely possible implication | 0.02–0.18 within a reading-supported scenario, ranked by best supporting/rival explanations |
| Source credibility | `clamp(0.2 + 0.65 * NPC trust - 0.35 * suspicion, 0.05, 0.95)`; own sources 1.0 |
| Multiple sources/routes | shared source states; average agreement over consistent states, permitting alternative proof routes |
| Reading accuracy | separate 0–1 inspector setting per report; defaults to 1, including own readings |
| Supplied test factor | 1/16–16 evil/good likelihood ratio; same-incident factors share corroborating witness routes |
| Player multiplier | `0.5 + trust in player` |
| Confidence multiplier | Original: `1.4 - 0.8 * confidence`; persistence experiment: 1 |
| Actual alignment effect | `0.45 * (evil evidence change - good evidence change)`, compared against private-evidence reference |
| Overlapping pattern route | `0.3 * pattern weight`; choose stronger absolute alignment/pattern route, not their sum |
| Outing pattern | 0.3 per distinct night, credibility-scaled; 0.2 for a conflicting alibi; capped at 0.8 |
| Gift explanation | reduce that outing's weight by `0.8 * credibility of supporting gift claim` |
| Stance | accept at 0.6 agreement; partial at 0.35; otherwise reject; actual numerical effect stays visible |

These are not calibrated probabilities or final balance recommendations. Accuracy sliders
do not simulate the causes of illness. Chosen assumptions get a provisional conditional
tier, not a full Bayesian posterior. Direct outing suspicion still uses the earlier pattern
heuristic and can now affect implicated people under any alignment headline. It is not
an inferred movement likelihood. Supplied test factors and the challenged-alibi factor
exercise convergence within the world comparison. A Marked headline is assessed, but
does not itself grant a fixed suspicion discount; only the underlying evidence's evil/good
consequences change suspicion. There is still
there is no separate learned-secret belief ledger. No public-pressure or vote-threshold
effects are simulated. Player trust is held fixed as an experimental input, so its shown
before/after change is zero. NPC responses use deterministic assessment text rather than
the production voice system.

One editable case has one replaceable vector ledger entry per listener. The old effects are
removed **before** assessing a revision, avoiding feedback through the case's own
accusation of a witness. Repetition, wrapper changes and venue changes cannot stack it.
Independent simultaneous cases and support graphs shared between them are future work.

## Verification

Run with the project's Godot executable:

```text
godot --headless --path . --script res://tools/test_workshop.gd
godot --headless --path . --script res://tools/test_workshop_cases.gd
godot --headless --path . --script res://tools/test_workshop_reasoning.gd
godot --headless --path . --script res://tools/test_workshop_ui.gd
godot --headless --path . --script res://tools/test_workshop_canvas.gd
godot --headless --path . --script res://tools/test_workshop_consequences.gd
godot --headless --path . --script res://tools/test_workshop_remaining.gd
godot --headless --path . res://ui/case_workshop.tscn -- --workshop-smoke
```

The semantic suite covers the mirror worlds, conditional choices, source-dependent
persuasion, retraction, duplicate evidence, distinct outings, gift explanations, differing
priors, confidence, hidden-truth independence, seeded coherence and JSON replay. The UI
suite exercises actual slot signals, duplicate prevention, explicit claim citations,
conflict scope, reasoning-line assignment, the factor control, remembered presentation,
save/open, reasoning/confidence controls, nonmutating comparisons, and opening/reopening
the Workshop without replacing the running game.
The new cases suite covers shared proof routes, localized doubt, neutral/contrary factors,
indirect Nisha-to-Constance support, conflicting/alternative line assumptions, same-incident
corroboration, wrapper identity, provenance and atomic import validation.
The reasoning suite covers paired Insight profiles, independent referee results,
bounded conviction and speech, ignored connections, direct private checks, confidence
revision, repeated presentations, no invented illness exceptions and exact theory replay.
The canvas suite covers equivalence of direct/chained readings, shared roots, nested
assumptions, quoted versus endorsed alibis, conditional circumstantial convergence,
merged provenance, cycles, real drag/link/answer/unlink signals, and canvas/history replay.
The consequences suite covers collateral implication/exoneration, scoped intermediate
claims, rejected inferences, nested claim-less groups, duplicate/shared branches,
order-independent effects, private facts, shared bounded attention, clamping/retraction,
exact replay after social-input edits, and the actual jury-window displays.

On the development machine (Intel i5-12400, Godot 4.7.2, Windows), a representative
single-juror full grouped assessment now includes every character and intermediate claim.
See the cases suite's printed cold/cached and 4,096-state broad-stress timings for current
measurements. Whole-jury assessment is synchronous, so broad stress cases can pause
the UI. These are fixture measurements, not general
campaign performance guarantees. Constraint and movement caches are bounded; credibility
weights are recomputed, so changing trust/reliability does not reuse stale weights.

For a rendered smoke test add `--workshop-smoke --screenshot <absolute-path.png>` after
the user-argument separator when launching the Workshop scene without `--headless`.

==> docs/CASE_WORKSHOP_CHECKPOINT.md <==
# Workshop checkpoint before live integration

The completed bounded proof of concept is commit **`5d30513`** on
`codex/case-workshop`. Its reading, movement, impairment and conversion experiments
are the reference, not a commitment to the present enumeration algorithm or weights.

## What survives a framework change

- The card-question interaction and graph: answer cards, cite original reports,
  connect intermediate claims, merge contradictions and revise a case.
- Evidence semantics: source attribution, original incidents, observation/report/learning
  times, quoted testimony versus endorsed contents, and uncertainty about health.
- Behavioral requirements: Bart can be Marked; coherent does not mean established;
  Hollis can be implicated without convincing the listener about Constance; repeated
  reports and overlapping branches cannot multiply the same evidence.
- Temporal requirements: conversion does not invalidate an earlier reading; withdrawal
  concerns the particular statement; restoration cannot restore erased evil knowledge.
- Revision requirements: remove the previous case's consequences before reassessment,
  retract collateral effects too, and do not let a case undermine its own sources on
  each repetition.

These discoveries are more portable than the numerical model. A new interpreter still
needs to preserve dependencies, uncertainty, temporal exceptions and revision behavior.
That is meaningful implementation work even when individual examples are easy to explain.

## Portable specimens

`tests/fixtures/workshop_checkpoint_v5.json` contains six frozen input/output examples:
the four experiments, the converging graph with intermediate claims, and Bart-as-Marked.
Only permitted evidence, case structure and listener settings are included. Generator
truth is absent. No world generation is needed to evaluate the saved examples.

Run `tools/test_workshop_checkpoint.gd` to compare the current interpreter with these
examples. Use `-- --record` only when intentionally replacing the reference. The saved
stance, search status, compatible-world count, claim responses and signed effects
document model v5. Matching its floating-point values is a migration aid, not a design
requirement for a deliberately different model. These six specimens supplement the
broader semantic suites; they are not a complete behavioral specification.

## Live boundary

Integration starts with `Game.workshop_snapshot(card_numbers, listener_id, venue)`.
`game/workshop_bridge.gd` collects permitted data; `workshop/live_evidence.gd` produces
the versioned, JSON-compatible `demonologist-live-evidence-1` contract. It contains no
World/NPC objects. Another interpreter can consume this contract without reproducing
the Workshop generator or depending on the UI.

The adapter reads selected original evidence and recursively follows deduction inputs.
It preserves separate testimony receipts, common outing incident IDs, public setup rules,
public events and the selected listener's private readings, observations and beliefs.
An evil listener uses its existing cover mind. Capture neither creates nor synchronizes
that mind, changes beliefs, advances RNGs, nor spends player actions.

Legacy data has limits: relay disclosure time is not the original reporting time;
same-day changed readings need distinct assertion identities; some scene/event details
exist only in displayed text. These are retained with explicit incomplete/unsupported
issues, not interpreted by parsing prose or filled from hidden causes. Content identity
is not proof of a shared original acquisition event. Direct witness report times are
first witnessed reports, not claims to know an earlier undisclosed statement.

Live rules permit four modifier kinds, whereas the fixture assumes one Marked person.
At the initial adapter checkpoint the transport therefore reports `assessment_ready:
false`. It does not feed live cases to the narrower fixture solver or apply additional
suspicion over the old reading ledger. Subsequent restricted comparison has its own
interpreter-specific `ready` result and resolves the modifier alternatives for supported
count-reading cases. Its current scope is documented in [the integration guide](CASE_WORKSHOP_INTEGRATION.md).
The original transport remains independent of any particular interpreter's capabilities.

==> docs/CASE_WORKSHOP_EXPERIMENTS.md <==
# Remaining Workshop experiments: movement and chronology

Slices 4 and 5 are implemented as bounded experiments. Open **More > Case Workshop**
and use the experiment selector beside **Make a Case**. The original 692 reading-chain
example remains available. Switching experiments starts a fresh workspace; save first
to retain an arrangement. These experiments do not alter the running main-game case.

## Movement & soot

The dawn-4 fixture adds a third legal attack, two crime-scene soot reports, two Market
evening observations, a further vigil, explicit observation coverage, a private-errand
claim, and a personal item. The existing reading ambiguity remains. Constance and Nisha
have declared movement hypotheses; this is not a general route planner for every role.

Present the supplied case, then unlink/relink the second soot/evening pair. Compare
the jury's **Effects on everyone** and calculation. Answer a sighting to cite an
applicable walking-gift claim or the private-errand claim. A Medium can only visit
eligible homes of people already dead; claiming Medium does not explain every route.
Nisha's Warden hypothesis has different eligible destinations. A complete vigil on the
Square cannot establish that someone stayed home elsewhere.

The likelihood sums over a shared activity profile and preferred evening haunt with
variable daily visits, then over legal
routes each night. Reports of the same outing are conjoined; copied facts do not become
new incidents. Distinct nights share the profile/haunt and are conditionally combined.
The model compares evil hypotheses (hunting Demon or minion) with good hypotheses
(unidentified lawful activity, claimed walking gift, private errand). Marked registration
is separate from alignment, and the Marked cannot simultaneously have another secret
modifier explaining outings. A claimed explanation changes a hypothesis mixture only
through an actually cited, credibility-gated statement; selecting a label alone does not.

The profile priors are **experimental**, not measured prevalence in the main game:

| Hypothesis | Prior / activity rule |
| --- | --- |
| Evil | 1/3 hunting Demon, 2/3 minion; the minion mixture walks with probability 1/4 |
| Good without supporting claim | .75 unidentified lawful activity, .15 walking gift, .10 private errand |
| Good with credible gift claim | .20 unidentified activity, .70 gift, .10 errand |
| Good with credible errand claim | .20 unidentified activity, .10 gift, .70 errand |
| Night activity | Hunt/gift 1 when eligible; private errand .55; residual lawful activity .05 |
| Preferred evening haunt | Uniform over four blocks; each evening visits it with probability .70, each other block .10 |
| Home account while out | Hunt/minion .70, private errand .65, gift .15, residual activity .20; home account while home .90 |

The residual lawful profile represents uncertainty about other legal roles/activities;
it is not a new ability added to ordinary townsfolk. Full role-assignment constraints,
shared criminal targets and a generative town-wide distribution are outside this fixture.

Two conditional mechanisms are checked against **10,000 calls to the actual game
generator**, rather than treating those priors as calibrated:

- A crime scene starts with the killer's evening material. If an eligible passer exists,
  the game selects one on a 40% branch; matching material leaves the trace unchanged.
  The experiment includes explicit alternative passer/background hypotheses.
- A walker drops something with probability .35 for the Demon or .15 for someone else;
  the item is personal with probability .12. Thus .042/.018 are conditional on walking,
  before route-block, location selection and search opportunity. Dropped material is
  from the walker's **home**; crime-scene material is from an **evening** location.

The inspector shows conditional good/evil likelihoods and their ratio, separately from
observation credibility and final suspicion. These are not probabilities of guilt.
Soft likelihoods never delete logical reading worlds. This experiment disables the
earlier outing heuristic and fixed alibi bonus: observations enter one shared model.
Challenged home accounts enter that same likelihood; a good private errand can also
explain concealment. The account probabilities above are experimental, not measured
lying rates and not a determination of deliberate deception.

The movement experiment also separates conviction from entailment. Its conviction
adjustment is twice the change in the best-support/best-rival preference fraction,
clamped to [-1, 1], over the original disposition score. A neutral or irrelevant
factor adds zero. Bounded jurors use only their checked candidates (or direct factor
odds when no rival was checked). This lets sufficiently strong circumstantial evidence
cross the acceptance threshold while a rival remains logically possible. It is still
experimental tuning, not a Bayesian probability of guilt. The signed alignment effect
is capped at ±.45 before player/confidence multipliers, and social multipliers apply once.
The original reading fixture retains its earlier provisional tuning for comparison.

## Poison & recovery

This fixture changes Hollis's actual minion role to Poisoner, scripts a legal Night-1
visit to Fenwick, updates Watch/Lamplighter observations, and calls the real Dowser
routine while poisoned and after recovery. The bad Day-2 report, its retelling and the
Day-3 recovered report retain different observation/reporting times.

Open **Answer** on the affected Dowser card. **Possible poisoning — unsupported** is
a hypothesis with no free reliability discount. Citing the vial observation opens one
shared healthy/poisoned uncertainty across reports within the interval, provided that
observation is also linked into the case. The vial supports poisoning, not certainty.
Remove it to withdraw that support. The recovered report and unrelated sightings retain
their own credibility.

Poison lasts from Night 1 until Night 2 starts, including the intervening day, matching
`World.resolve_night`. The experiment supports only its declared interval/subject;
arbitrary poison histories are rejected as unsupported. The health branch is 50/50
conditional on accepting the supporting witness, an exposed experimental prior. Witness
credibility is shared with that source's other evidence. Retellings of one reading share
an accuracy origin as well as the poison episode, rather than creating independent tests.

## Revenant & restoration

This fixture exercises `World._revenant_curse` and `World.find_totem`/`free_turned`.
Merrin is the scripted developer-truth victim, but that identity never enters the
assessor. The public chronology states only that one originally-good surviving yes-voter
was converted: Merrin, Nisha or Odette. Activation also establishes that the executed
person was the evil Revenant. The native restoration clears evil intel and disowns an
actual fake report recorded during conversion.

Answer the warning card to propose **who became evil when the Revenant was executed**.
Answer a premise to choose **before curse**, **during curse**, or **after restoration**.
The proposal narrows the player's explanation; it is not new evidence identifying the
converted person. Dated assumptions can say Merrin was good before and evil during
without creating a false contradiction. The premise card displays its epoch.

Reliable reports constrain source alignment at the time of the reading. Credibility of
a later retelling is keyed to its reporting period. Consequently an earlier healthy
reading survives later conversion. A publicly withdrawn retelling is disabled only if
the withdrawal is in that listener's permitted knowledge; the original reading remains.
The good-cover restoration adapter accepts only explicit permitted fields and filters
withdrawn own retellings. Evil-only intel is not copied.

The public event sequence is fixed and known to this fixture's jurors. Uncertain event
occurrence, unobserved restoration, multiple curses and full NPC memory simulation are
not implemented. The Workshop still revises one case/effect vector at a time; changing
the queried epoch replaces that vector rather than maintaining a complete historical
belief database. Scion succession has a reserved schema name and remains unsupported,
as specified in the plan. There is no invented misremembering exception.

## Replay, testing and performance

Version **5** exports identify the fixture and preserve chronology, fault hypotheses,
dated assumptions, canvas layout and presentation history. Old model histories are
explicitly refused. Outcomes are reconstructed from inputs, never accepted as imported
NPC beliefs. Native generation is deterministic and hidden causes remain optional
developer data.

Run `tools/test_workshop_remaining.gd` alongside the six earlier suites. It exercises
the actual selector, poison answer, converted-voter answer, premise epoch control,
presentation and canvas save/open for all three new fixtures. The test prints whole-jury
latency, cold/cached model assessment, state/search/assignment counts and separate
healthy-report conflict-extraction time. Ranked-world, constraint and movement caches use bounded entries and exact
input checks; time, eligibility, observations, credibility and model changes invalidate
the relevant results.

The Workshop latency budget is **five seconds for the supplied four-juror experiments**
on the development machine (i5-12400, Godot 4.7.2, Windows). Tests report actual timing
rather than asserting a hardware-sensitive deadline. Larger uncertainty stress cases
remain synchronous and may exceed this budget. This is not sufficient evidence for
production latency; integration needs separate workloads, pruning and a stricter budget.

The next work is a separate [main-game integration plan](CASE_WORKSHOP_INTEGRATION.md),
not further automatic expansion of these fixtures into a campaign simulator.

==> docs/CASE_WORKSHOP_INTEGRATION.md <==
# Main-game integration

The bounded Workshop experiments are the reference implementation, not a replacement
already running in the main game. Integrate in verified vertical steps:

The pre-integration reference is commit `5d30513`. The [checkpoint and portable
specimens](CASE_WORKSHOP_CHECKPOINT.md) describe what must survive an interpreter change.
The read-only `Game.workshop_snapshot` API provides a portable evidence contract,
capability issues and hidden-state isolation checks. A restricted live comparison now
consumes that contract for Surveyor, Rumormonger and Dowser cases. An experimental shared
owner can now replace certified legacy suspicion for that restricted evidence set. It is
off by default and can be enabled in the live canvas without Debug, or in the developer
inspector. Stages 1–4 and a restricted live canvas are implemented within the reading
scope below. Broader mechanic adapters and ordinary multi-case playtesting remain.

## Live canvas

**More → Case Workshop** opens the live editor. A board card's right-click menu also
offers **Add to Case Workshop**, which opens and selects that original receipt. This
first version preloads all non-deduction board evidence; hidden cards are player-known
and remain available. Native filed deductions are not imported as graph nodes: their
old flat form cannot recover answers or branch semantics. The old fixture canvas is
still available through `ui/case_workshop.tscn` with F6.

1. Add a premise, answer supported readings, and link evidence or supporting premises.
   Disconnected cards are scratch work. Dragging and layout supply no evidence.
2. Inspect the **whole-case support label**. Enable **Experimental reading evaluator**
   to apply the new restricted model for this case; opening the editor alone leaves it off.
   This switch does not enable spoilers or disable identification scoring.
3. **Preview effectiveness** is a manual, read-only neutral reference. It uses the same
   full graph and live capability gate as presentation. Unsupported cases have no new-
   model numeric preview and no claimed prediction of the native fallback.
4. **File / update case** retains the graph as a native board deduction with an exact
   original-receipt mapping. It preserves answers, assumptions, intermediate claims,
   branch-removal provenance, quoted conflicts and all future unsupported choices.
   Filing alone changes no listener beliefs. Each premise keeps its filed board number
   across revisions; a listener replaces the old endorsement only upon hearing the revision.
5. Present during an actual conversation, meeting or nomination. The button uses the
   current context, not an unrestricted mock audience. Private presentations consume
   normal questions; the floor remains once per meeting; nomination speech remains one
   statement with normal social effects. Accuse/defend chooses the social stance toward
   the nominee; it does not rewrite the case's headline.
6. The result window names each delivered listener's **new shared evaluator** or
   **fallback: existing rules**. Listener-dependent incompatibility is reported without
   exposing their private evidence or alignment. Rejected/expired actions give no
   delivery record and direct the player to the ordinary conversation log.

The capability label covers Surveyor/Rumormonger/Dowser readings at supported acquisition
times, original-alignment claims, selected answers and scoped identity assumptions. It
does not promise that every listener can combine them with their existing knowledge:
source/receipt ownership, credibility budgets and public temporal history can require
fallback. Numerical shared effects use the original reports once; chosen answers are
hypotheses assessed in the scoped response, never additional facts or extra bonuses.

**Unsupported evidence is usable. It is never silently omitted from an asserted case.**
Every unsupported card is labeled, and including it flags the whole connected case.
Movement, trace, alibi disputes, additional role claims/home cards as separate premises,
outing explanations/citations, impairment hypotheses, changed-alignment histories and
composing a canvas graph inside a separate legacy deduction remain outside the new
live evaluator. Even a linked role claim that seems redundant is explicitly flagged.
Reading conflicts presented as the headline are supported without assigning guilt.
Embedded conflict questions remain flagged: ownership can distinguish quotation from
endorsement, but the scoped response's attention needs a quoted-evidence adapter before
it can assess them together with a different headline. Present the conflict separately
to use the new evaluator. Live movement/alibi conflicts also fall back and do not inherit
the fixture's experimental 2× factor.

The fallback uses the **headline plus original cards** through the existing argument
system. It does **not** interpret canvas answers, assumed identities, intermediate
claims or link meanings. This loss is stated before presentation, and actual fallback
is labeled afterward. A mixed inseparable case is not partially evaluated by dropping
unsupported evidence. An absent/deleted/changed original receipt blocks filing instead
of submitting fewer roots. Independent reasoning lines with different evaluators are
not implemented yet.

Draft layouts/answers and filed-number mappings are retained in memory only for the
running case, including closing/reopening the window and refreshing new board evidence.
Starting a new case, even the same seed, clears them and resets effects off. There is
no live draft disk export/import in this slice. **Withdraw endorsement…** is explicitly
a developer testing tool: it retracts that case's shared/native canvas contribution for
the selected listener while retaining independently learned evidence. It is not an
in-character retraction conversation or a reversal of past trust/social effects.

Town presentations yield between listeners and show progress. Individual assessments
remain synchronous. Other game commands and a new-case action are locked until the
presentation finishes, keeping one ordinary command/trace boundary. Typed presentations
still use the synchronous command handler. A ten-person native-generated reading case
took approximately 29 seconds for the first town presentation on the development machine;
this is a measured limitation, not the earlier one-listener timing extrapolated to town.

`workshop_canvas_filed` traces retain the full submitted graph and resolved board roots;
`workshop_delivery` records actual model/fallback feedback for every recipient, and
`workshop_presentation` retains shared assessments, novelty and ownership. The live canvas
suite covers native-generated gifts, private → meeting → nomination → execution/night,
floor/question/statement limits, progress action locking, mixed-case fallback/recovery,
stable revisions, missing receipts, main-menu/context entry points and case resets.

## Try the live comparison

Open **More → Live Workshop comparison** in the main game. To inspect private NPC
responses, enable the existing **Debug** control; its normal identification-scoring
warning still applies. Choose a filed deduction and listener, then **Inspect now**.
The optional recording checkbox also captures comparisons when actual deductions are
presented privately, to the meeting, or during nomination speech. All three venues use
one comparison adapter. The detailed trace stores the captured input, actual named
presentation model, read-only proposal, capability issues and elapsed time. With shared
effects disabled, the actual result is the original legacy result; with them enabled it
can be the applied shared assessment. No hypothetical legacy result is fabricated. Recording is off by
default and adds work only when enabled; the panel keeps the most recent 24 records.
Each comparison also audits overlapping legacy reading entries and potentially
dependent arguments. Distinct receipts sharing an old source/day/role key are exposed
as ambiguous identities. Ordinary testimony is not treated as a gift-reading dependency.
Ambiguous original reading identities defer numeric comparison. These diagnostics do
not grant permission to delete or suppress any old entry.

The live model enumerates legal original alignment worlds and all four public modifier
kinds. Non-Marked worlds have no Marked person; Lovers have two good holders. Accepted
healthy readings exclude a Delusional source. Healthy role claims must be compatible
with unique gifts and the listener's own known gift. Modifier identities and actual
poison/curse targets are never read from the simulation for inference.
The listener retains public known-good Demon victims and Martyr participants even if
their process cards are not cited. Working-gift branches respect first-night-only
Surveyor/Rumormonger acquisition and daytime Dowser acquisition.

One native exception matters: Rumormonger excludes itself when choosing a modifier
holder. If it is the sole holder, a working gift can generate an arbitrary fallback
pair/secret. The live model retains that explanation without establishing the reported
pair or modifier type. This is tested against `_receive_info`, not inferred from a
handwritten special-case fixture. The fixed Workshop validator's assumption that report
contents are accurate is stronger than merely having a working gift in this situation.
Changing the native fallback is recorded as a separate open backlog decision.

Comparisons are conditional on report credibility and health, use full reasoning, and
retain the Workshop's experimental weights. They support up to eight reading receipts,
six claims and 64 credibility combinations for seven- or ten-person towns. Nested filed
deductions retain their original evidence and scoped intermediate claims. Unsupported
roles, live movement/trace likelihoods, incomplete payloads and public conversion history
produce explicit non-numeric results. The complete live health/poison history is not
modeled. Report, observation and learning times remain distinct; original report time
can remain unknown when a legacy relay receipt does not record it.

Venue is recorded but the comparison proposal is unscaled; the real original result keeps
its actual venue scaling and social side effects. Read-only comparison proposals use the
captured existing beliefs, which can contain legacy reading effects: they must not be
added directly. Comparison recording never applies a proposal. Shared ownership below
uses a separate, cleaned baseline and total-evidence assessment. Existing evil cover minds are
captured without creating or syncing them; a missing cover mind yields an unsupported
result. Comparisons remain a developer tool rather than an alternate gameplay mode.

Tests exercise public legal domains, all modifier types, distinct world/cache keys,
healthy unique gifts, hidden-truth independence, no preview mutation, original behavior
and RNG equivalence across all venues, JSON checkpoint replay and the actual inspector.
The initial one-reading benchmark was roughly 0.2 seconds for seven people and 1.6
seconds for ten on this machine; that is not a whole-town or large-case latency claim.
See [performance and migration options](CASE_WORKSHOP_PERFORMANCE.md).

## Shared ownership and retraction (experimental)

In **More → Live Workshop comparison**, enable Debug and select **Experimental shared
reading effects** to apply the supported joint model. This is a per-case developer
experiment and resets off for a new case. The switch recomputes existing supported raw
readings; subsequent raw readings and deduction presentations refresh automatically.
Private, meeting-floor and nomination deductions register the same endorsement identity.
Disabling retracts the shared layer and restores the native ledger calculation.

`workshop/shared_ledger.gd` is the portable ownership planner. `Mind.apply_info` now
records normalized original receipts; each legacy argument justification records its exact
original roots and board-case identity. Relays, reordered groups, duplicate board wrappers,
different conclusions and different venues do not create extra original facts. The legacy
coarse source/day/role id remains intact for compatibility; distinct statements colliding
under it cause explicit fallback rather than silently certifying one another.

The owner assesses the union of independently learned reports and active player case
endorsements once. It moves private reports into this total-evidence union while retaining
self/own-role and public known-good constraints. The earlier comparison's subtraction of
private evidence is deliberately not used as the total learned-evidence effect. Intermediate
labels supply no new constraints or bonus; the actual presented case's scoped diagnostics
remain available separately in the read-only comparison.

The baseline excludes the entire previous shared vector and certified legacy suspicion
effects. Those legacy grounds are also excluded from the honesty passes, preventing a
second credibility penalty from the same accusation. Native interval doubt, protection and
spare preferences, and role-belief effects retain their original owners. Joint source
credibility comes from independent baseline suspicion and NPC trust; this slice does not
feed joint accusations back into native honesty, which would require another explicit
credibility model. Supported presentation replies, acceptance and sound-argument rewards
now use a scoped assessment with the same clean baseline, report weights, background
evidence and world rules as the applied union. Intermediate acceptance can be explained
without accepting the headline. Strategic evil masking, ordinary conversation fatigue,
public nomination support/hostility and voting remain separate social systems.

Player trust discounts reports whose only route is player presentation (factor
`clamp(0.5 + trust, 0, 1)` in report acceptance). Independently learned readings instead
retain their legacy hearing weight (capped at 1), together with source credibility and
interval reliability. They do not globally become stronger or weaker with player trust.
The aggregate uses neutral player scaling and the listener's existing confidence scaling.
These are experimental interpretation weights, not calibrated guilt probabilities.

Re-presenting a revised board case replaces that case's exclusive roots. **Withdraw
selected endorsement** removes one case path for the selected listener and suppresses
its retired native argument contribution. Facts remain if another case or an independent
reading still owns them. Withdrawal means withdrawing endorsement, not making the NPC
forget independently learned testimony. This control is a debugging operation; it is not
yet an in-character conversation command. Turning the whole experiment off restores the
native system, including native arguments that had been withdrawn in the experimental
discussion.

An outside-suspicion layer preserves independent later changes and their clamps while
joint effects are replaced or withdrawn. Ordinary native adjustments, including paranoia
drift, update that outside layer. Pre-existing legacy contributions that were clipped, or
owned legacy targets already at a boundary without a reversible baseline, are declined
rather than guessing how much to retract. Direct absolute native assignments are reconciled
at the next reassessment. Public conversion/restoration history causes complete fallback;
cover routing never supplies actual evil intelligence to the interpreter. A turned mind's
dormant layer is retained until cover creation or restoration; if it was freed before a
cover existed, native effects are restored before the post-restoration suspicion cap.

Independent unsupported readings retain native ownership and contribute to the outside
baseline; their presence no longer disables unrelated supported reports. Exact original
receipts now identify private card accusations, defences, group presentations and public
nomination cards, including reports heard before enabling the experiment. Those paths
share one report owner with raw readings and filed deductions. Citing a deduction as a
card follows its original roots rather than treating its conclusion as a new fact.
Supported private accusations remain in ordinary NPC gossip; a stale relayed deduction
does not silently borrow a later revision from the player's board.

Inseparable mixed or uncertified argument paths, unresolved native receipt collisions,
old deduction wrappers lacking roots, unsupported selected evidence, unsupported readings
sharing a source with owned reports, temporal transitions
and workloads above the eight-report/64-state budget still restore the legacy calculation
where ownership cannot be established. Original entries are kept, not deleted. Missing
adapters appear in the inspector's ownership status and detailed trace. General live
movement/trace/health/transition semantics are still future work.

## Unified presentations and quoted conflicts

`game/workshop_presentation.gd` handles supported deduction and raw-card presentations
through the same good-mind callback. Raw accusations ask whether the target is suspicious;
defences ask whether they are good. Group presentations retain separately assessed
questions and one joint evidence effect. Private, meeting and nomination venues preserve
interpretation. Silence suppresses speech without suppressing reasoning. Evil's cover
receives repetitions, revised cases and self-targeting allegations; the real evil mind
keeps its strategic mask and receives no inferred suspicion or sound-argument trust.

Accepted presentations can earn the existing +0.02 sound-argument trust reward when they
offer new original reports. Rewrapping, changing targets, reordering roots or changing
venue cannot repeatedly reward those reports. Ordinary question fatigue is unchanged.
Semantic repetition and audience delivery are distinct: a new audience gets an explanation
without another evidence award; a repeated explanation to the same audience is shortened.

A conflict presents evidence that the statements were made, without endorsing both
contents. Quoted roots stay separate from the endorsed union. If another path independently
endorses one of those reports, that report retains its existing owner and weight. A nested
conflict remains an assessed intermediate question without sneaking its contradictory
contents into the parent's alignment premises.
Independent direct endorsement of a report survives when that report also occurs in a
quoted child. Branch-removal diagnostics retain roots still supplied by another path.
Healthy report constraints still allow
Marked to register differently on different readings; apparent conflicts with such an
explanation are not called impossible.

Recognizing a conflict adds no guilt and invents no all-night impairment for its sources.
The parked legacy conflict's broad doubt and suspicion effects are suppressed while the
shared owner is active. A supported conflict accepted at agreement >= 0.6 can put an
actually cited participant under scrutiny once (+0.1 privately, +0.2 publicly); rejected
conflicts and repeated wrappers/venues add no such pressure. Merely putting someone's
name in the conflict group does not make them a cited source. This scrutiny is a social
consequence, not an alignment inference.

Substantial joint suspicion can be cited as coming from the reports considered together.
Disclosure is conservative: private presentations are citable to the player, public
presentations/disclosures to the town. Later private restatements do not erase public
knowledge of those reports. Unknown private roots remain unpublished. A joint
reason does not trigger the old own-gift reveal/hint machinery, and an owned legacy bonus
that has been retracted cannot remain as the stated reason. Source health and honesty
are still hypotheses, not public assertions established by this generic explanation.

Compatible native entries are parked using the shared assessment's agreement so a later
capability fallback has a contribution to restore. They are not a simulation of what the
old argument interpreter would have concluded in a separate playthrough. Preferences and
role beliefs retain native ownership. Withdrawal also removes direct citations of the
selected filed deduction; independently learned raw-card reports remain known.

`workshop_presentation` traces separate agreement, scoped intermediate assessments,
response, original novelty, quoted receipts, trust change, scrutiny and the total applied
union. Read-only inspector records explicitly name whether the actual response was shared
or legacy. Joint effects and response calculations cache one exact pure input per good/
cover listener, reset on a new case. Baseline, role knowledge, report weights, trust,
interval doubt, evidence and public chronology invalidate the relevant calculation.
Retraction restores an unchanged outside baseline exactly; it does not add and subtract
the same floating-point value in a way that slowly changes priors or cache keys.

## Manual effectiveness preview

The live canvas, fixture canvas and live comparison panel have **Preview effectiveness**.
It calculates on demand, never on every link, and does not change the case, remembered
mock-jury discussion, NPCs, time, RNG or identification scoring. Live preview is available
without Debug and uses selected player-held evidence plus public process events only.

The ghost is a new reference profile with no town identity, self-good assumption, private
reports, known gift, actual NPC trust or hidden truth. Its fixed inputs are player/witness
trust 0.5, initial suspicion 0.1 and confidence 0.5 with full reasoning. The result reports
approximate signed effects for all people, intermediate acceptance/remaining alternatives,
and conditional compatible assignments. Unsupported evidence yields an explicit explanation.
These values describe the case under declared neutral assumptions; they predict neither
a particular NPC nor a vote. Assignment counts are not probabilities of guilt.

## Integration sequence and remaining work

Stages 1–4 now have paired tests, including raw/case overlap, revisions, repeated venues,
independent learning, source-doubt recovery, clamping/retraction, native preference and
role effects, cover isolation, capability fallback and neutral preview. The frozen fixture
checkpoint remains unchanged. Full-case general-role rollout and response parity remain
future gates, not evidence supplied by these restricted tests. The live canvas now files
and presents its full answered graph through those paths. The separate F6 fixture UI
remains the reference for mechanics not yet adapted to a running case.

Run `godot --headless --path . --script res://tools/test_workshop_ownership.gd` for the
57 ownership/ghost checks. `test_workshop_presentation.gd` adds presentation, mixed-owner,
quoted-conflict, source privacy, intermediate acceptance and cache checks, alongside `test_workshop_integration.gd`,
`test_workshop_live_ui.gd`, `test_workshop_canvas.gd`, native argument/belief/cover tests,
`test_workshop_live_canvas.gd` and `test_trace.gd`. The ownership suite includes actual turn/free lifecycle calls with
no cover, tiny legacy remnants below the usual display threshold, clipped-migration
refusal, and over-budget fallback. UI tests exercise the manual preview without Debug.

1. **Live evidence adapter, read-only.** Map board reports, testimony and observations
   to stable original fact/incident identities, keeping observation, reporting and learned
   time distinct. Construct each listener's permitted knowledge; never pass role truth,
   the actual poison target or conversion identity as an inference shortcut. Record
   unsupported mechanics explicitly instead of silently excluding legal alternatives.
2. **Restricted live comparison.** Choose cases whose roles, registration, faults and
   public chronology are supported. Evaluate without changing the NPC. Trace original
   evidence, intermediate acceptance, considered alternatives, likelihood assumptions
   and proposed consequences beside the old assessment. Validate ordinary playthroughs
   as well as the 692 teaching example. Profile realistic town and evidence sizes.
3. **One source of belief effects.** Reconcile existing `Mind.apply_info` reading effects,
   `Argue.evaluate` deductions and new joint consequences. Use replacement or incremental
   inference over a shared evidence store; do not add the new vector over old bonuses
   for the same reports. Share provenance across multiple player cases, NPC discoveries
   and retellings. Recompute affected consequences when a source or interval is doubted.
4. **One semantic presentation path.** Route private presentation, the meeting floor and
   nomination speech through that assessment. Audience, disclosure, pressure and voting
   can differ. Venue changes and repeated presentations must not award fresh evidence.
   Adapt good-cover reasoning for evil NPCs without leaking real evil knowledge.
5. **Controlled playable rollout.** Enable new effects for a supported subset, retain
   old/new diagnostics, and inspect persuasion, social pressure and voting separately.
   Preserve other effects such as protection preferences and role beliefs until their
   replacements exist. Run argument/belief regressions and full-case save/replay tests.
6. **Expand mechanic coverage.** Define each additional role/event's constraints and
   legitimate exceptions, then test combinations. Do not enumerate bespoke argument
   chains. Extend the causal movement model, event uncertainty, Scion succession and
   multiple histories only with appropriate generators and tests.

Acceptance includes no hidden-truth dependence, no duplicate awards across raw readings
and cases, coherent retraction, source-time correctness, honest unsupported/incomplete
feedback, agreement on intermediate claims without forced acceptance of their parent,
and playable response latency. The Workshop's five-second testing budget and hand-set
profile priors are not production targets or final balance recommendations.

==> docs/CASE_WORKSHOP_PERFORMANCE.md <==
# Reasoning performance and migration options

Recorded October 22, 2026. These are engineering options and priorities, not measured
speedup promises. The player currently presents a large town-wide case roughly once
per day (about every 5–10 minutes of play); a few seconds at that boundary may be
acceptable. Keep ordinary interaction responsive and measure realistic cases before
changing the model to meet an unnecessarily strict frame-time target.

## What drives cost

`Session.present` currently assesses listeners sequentially. Shared caches reuse some
legal worlds, report matches and movement likelihoods, but each listener has different
private knowledge, source trust, prior suspicion and attention. More listeners add
roughly linear listener-specific work when everything else is fixed. That is not a
benchmark guarantee: caches and different listener knowledge change the slope.

The credibility mixture enumerates `2^u` combinations for `u` uncertain assumptions.
Twelve uncertainties mean 4,096 combinations; sixteen mean 65,536. Doubling listeners
roughly doubles that portion; four more uncertainties can multiply it by sixteen.
Those effects multiply. Bounded NPC hypothesis search does not by itself bound the
outer credibility mixture. More townspeople also enlarge possible-world searches;
adding an existing listener and adding a character to the town are different costs.

Repeated per-person good/evil projections, intermediate claims, minimal conflicts and
branch-removal diagnostics add further work. Profile them separately. As of the
pre-integration checkpoint, supplied four-juror experiments took about 1.5–3.5 seconds
on the development machine; a broad 4,096-state one-juror stress test took about nine
seconds. These are development observations, not production or other-hardware promises.

## Preserve behavior before reducing precision

1. Reuse an assessment across character questions and cache stable normalized inputs.
   Prepare IDs, report constraints and provenance once; avoid repeated dictionary
   copying, string serialization and filtering inside inner loops. Dependency-aware
   invalidation should recompute only what a case or belief revision affects.
2. Compute expensive explanation diagnostics on demand: every minimal contradiction
   set and every branch-removal comparison are useful tools, not prerequisites for
   updating beliefs. Keep clear ordinary explanations without calculating every
   possible explanatory breakdown at presentation time.
3. If needed, replace exhaustive credibility combinations with bounded weighted
   assessment. This sacrifices numerical precision before argument expressiveness.
   Reuse deterministic selections across repeats/revisions; unchanged cases must not
   produce random attitude jitter. Refine near consequential acceptance/vote thresholds.
4. Check important alternatives explicitly, including Bart-as-Marked, health failure
   and relevant dated conversion. A rare exception must not disappear merely because
   a sampler misses it. Separate approximation of weights from logical validation.
   Failure to find a rival within a budget is not proof that none exists.
5. Keep collateral effects and intermediate claims. Limit work by evidence dependencies,
   not only the headline target. Do not split connected branches as independent when
   global counts or shared sources couple them. Preserve provenance and full retraction.

## C# migration

Port the measured computational core incrementally; keep the UI and ordinary game
orchestration in GDScript. The modules, portable checkpoint examples and live evidence
contract provide boundaries. A prototype hotspot port is a bounded experiment; a full
behavior-equivalent core port is a meaningful implementation effort. Temporal rules,
shared dependencies and revision semantics are more difficult than translating syntax.

Use typed .NET structures, integer person IDs, arrays/bitsets and ordinary .NET
collections internally. Convert evidence in and results out at a coarse boundary.
Directly translating every dynamic Godot Dictionary access can retain much of the
overhead: Godot collections require interop/marshalling from C#.

A several-fold numerical-core improvement is plausible but unmeasured here. Whole-call
benefit depends on the accelerated fraction: hypothetically accelerating 90% of a call
by 10x gives about 5.3x overall, not 10x. Faster code does not remove exponential growth.
Benchmark equivalent inputs, answers, cold/warm caches, allocations and exported builds.
Godot's .NET edition is required. Current Godot documentation excludes C# web export;
recheck that platform constraint before committing if browser distribution is intended.

## Other routes

The shared-ledger integration now recomputes an enabled listener's entire supported
evidence union after ledger changes and before reconsideration/voting. Profile those
refresh counts as well as per-assessment time: a presentation can invoke more than one
legacy ledger update. Exact whole-assessment and scoped-response caches now keep the last
pure input per good/cover listener, reset with the case; unchanged ledger refreshes reuse
the calculation. A supported presentation also avoids the caller's formerly redundant
final refresh. Tests change source trust and restore it to verify invalidation/recovery.
The eight-reading/64-state gate bounds supported work and falls back coherently rather
than truncating facts. More incremental invalidation/coalescing remains a candidate if
refresh overhead dominates. Ghost effectiveness previews
are explicitly requested with a button; editing links never launches them automatically.

One controlled ten-person, three-reading grouped case measured about 4.2 seconds for
its first listener assessment and 3 milliseconds for an unchanged repeated presentation
after exact baseline restoration. This is one case/listener on the development machine,
not a whole-town guarantee. Other listeners' knowledge/priors and newly changed cases
still require their own calculations. The presentation suite prints current cold/repeat
timings and checks exact input reuse, not a machine-specific timing threshold.

- Run pure assessment off the UI thread so the game stays responsive. This alone need
  not shorten the computation. Use immutable snapshots, separate/synchronized caches,
  cancellation and stale-result protection; do not access the active scene tree from
  worker calculations. Parallel listeners may reduce elapsed time within core/memory
  limits, but cannot simply share today's mutable caches unsafely.
- Compact constraint representations, pruning and dependency-aware inference can
  improve scaling without changing the game rules. A specialized constraint solver
  is a future benchmark candidate, not a promise of free weighted/temporal inference.
- A native C++/GDExtension core remains available if a measured C# core is insufficient.
  It adds build/distribution complexity and still needs sensible search budgets.
- Scheduling work during natural presentation/response pauses is preferable to
  sacrificing meaningful player expression solely for instantaneous answers.

The live-canvas smoke test now measures the whole town using native-generated readings
in a ten-person case. The first floor presentation took about **29 seconds** (10 listener
records), compared with the earlier ~4.2-second single-listener synthetic chain. The
live canvas yields and updates a progress dialog between listeners, with other game
